    "originalFile": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels matching correct within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$). The scrambled-correct equivalence could reflect classification regularizing representations independently of label content, or random permutation over 9 classes preserving enough correlations; distinguishing these requires varying class count. Topic classification occasionally approaches MLM on ActorAttack (0.625 vs.\\ 0.893) despite misaligned labels, reflecting domain exposure rather than the topic objective.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}\nToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 (+53\\,pp) while MLM collapses; one hypothesis is that MLM captures LLM-specific surface patterns while classification extracts style-invariant features. Cross-LLM error decomposition confirms F1 degradation is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text, not missed attacks.\n\n\\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}\nClassification supervision reduces variance 4--20$\\times$ over MLM on synthetic OOD (3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013).\nFrozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2: frozen MLM outperforms the full pipeline on ActorAttack (0.941 vs.\\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen matches (0.972; Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}).\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder capacity, temporal, and lexical analysis.}\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. Mean pooling matches BiGRU and shuffling produces $|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}), confirming order-invariant detection. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD) demonstrates beyond-lexical features (Section~\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nClassification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders; unsupervised pretraining closes $\\sim$60\\,pp but collapses under mixed-LLM training. Within the architectures tested, larger encoders need less adaptation; 4--20$\\times$ variance reduction reverses on human data. Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter. Continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative for multi-turn safety monitoring given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn human validation.}\nAll multi-turn findings are derived from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists for evaluation. Whether the evidence hierarchy and classification supervision advantages generalize to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question.\n\n\\paragraph{Turn-count distribution.}\nBenign conversations contain 12--20 turns, DD jailbreaks 6--10, and ActorAttack jailbreaks 3--8; the three categories have non-overlapping turn counts. Three observations argue against length as the primary detection cue: (a) early detection at equal-length prefixes $K$=1 and $K$=2 achieves AA OOD F1\\,=\\,0.773 and 0.935 (Appendix~\\ref{tab:actorattack_full}); (b) vanilla models access the same length signal but achieve near-zero OOD recall; and (c) truncating benign conversations to 3 turns yields FPR\\,=\\,0\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal aggregation dilution.}\nWhen subtle attacks (ActorAttack) are followed by extended benign padding, BiGRU temporal aggregation dilutes attack signals (F1 drops from 0.963 to 0.383; Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}); in practice, adversaries would not dilute their own successful attacks.\n\n\\paragraph{OOD benign distribution.}\nAll OOD evaluations hold the benign distribution constant (Qwen3-8B generated); results reflect attack-side generalization. On 226 WildChat benign conversations, all variants produce FPR\\,$\\geq$\\,44\\%, with 9-class worst at 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). Even with matched benign distributions, residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\n\\paragraph{Frozen encoder assumption.}\nThe frozen Stage-1 design isolates representation quality but may underestimate full-pipeline performance. End-to-end fine-tuning closes this gap to $\\leq$5.8\\,pp on ActorAttack (F1\\,=\\,0.997$\\pm$0.005 vs.\\ 0.939$\\pm$0.047 frozen; Table~\\ref{tab:e2e}; Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}).\n\n\\paragraph{Dataset scale and cross-LLM transfer.}\nScale validation (1{,}000 conversations; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_validation}) confirms the hierarchy holds (DAPT advantage 40--56\\,pp over vanilla). Cross-LLM validation shows jailbreak detection transfers when benign distribution is held constant (F1 recovers to 0.800--1.000), but benign-side F1 drops to 0.370--0.430 with unfamiliar benign text (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}). The perturbation evaluation ($n$=76) remains underpowered.\n\n\\paragraph{Strategy labels.}\nPersuasion strategy labels ($n$=50 human validation, $\\kappa$=0.78) are produced by Qwen3-8B; the validation sample yields small per-class sizes across 9 categories, limiting reliability estimates for individual strategies. OOD strategy accuracy degrades to 30.7\\% ($\\kappa$=0.126); interpretability is limited to known attack families. We plan to release annotation prompts and data for independent verification.\n\n\\paragraph{Detection architecture.}\nBinary vs.\\ 9-class equivalence (F1 gap $\\leq$3.9\\,pp) suggests the binary head is not the critical differentiator. FITD provides minimal discriminative signal (all variants F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.990), serving as confirmation that simple attacks do not challenge any variant.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder scale.}\nWhether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open; same-architecture scaling (e.g., DeBERTa-base vs.\\ DeBERTa-large) is needed to attribute the capacity effect to scale alone.\n\n\\paragraph{Scope and single-turn transfer.}\nOnly persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. ToxicChat performance reflects zero-shot single-turn transfer; doubling the training corpus drops ToxicChat macro F1 from 0.596 to 0.341$\\pm$0.004 while multi-turn OOD remains stable, confirming the architecture targets multi-turn detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Computation cost.}\nTotal pipeline cost for all seven variants is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",

Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter. Continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative for multi-turn safety monitoring given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).",

704

Strategy labels. Persuasion strategy labels
(n=50 human validation, κ=0.78) are produced
by Qwen3-8B; the validation sample yields small
per-class sizes across 9 categories, limiting reliability estimates for individual strategies. OOD
strategy accuracy degrades to 30.7% (κ=0.126); interpretability is limited to known attack families.
We plan to release annotation prompts and data for
independent verification.

generated by Qwen3-8B. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18–
42 pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multiturn jailbreak dataset exists for evaluation. Whether
the evidence hierarchy and classification supervision advantages generalize to human-authored
multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question.
Turn-count distribution. Benign conversations
contain 12–20 turns, DD jailbreaks 6–10, and ActorAttack jailbreaks 3–8; the three categories have
non-overlapping turn counts. Three observations
argue against length as the primary detection cue:
(a) early detection at equal-length prefixes K=1
and K=2 achieves AA OOD F1 = 0.773 and 0.935
(Appendix 8); (b) vanilla models access the same
length signal but achieve near-zero OOD recall;
and (c) truncating benign conversations to 3 turns
yields FPR = 0% (Appendix T).
Temporal aggregation dilution. When subtle
attacks (ActorAttack) are followed by extended benign padding, BiGRU temporal aggregation dilutes
attack signals (F1 drops from 0.963 to 0.383; Appendix T); in practice, adversaries would not dilute
their own successful attacks.
OOD benign distribution. All OOD evaluations hold the benign distribution constant (Qwen38B generated); results reflect attack-side generalization. On 226 WildChat benign conversations, all variants produce FPR ≥ 44%, with 9class worst at 68.1% (Appendix O). Even with
matched benign distributions, residual calibration
error (ECE = 0.126–0.141; Appendix Q) suggests
temperature scaling is needed for deployment.
Frozen encoder assumption. The frozen Stage-1
design isolates representation quality but may underestimate full-pipeline performance. End-to-end
fine-tuning closes this gap to ≤5.8 pp on ActorAttack (F1 = 0.997±0.005 vs. 0.939±0.047 frozen;
Table 17; Appendix R).
Dataset scale and cross-LLM transfer. Scale
validation (1,000 conversations; Appendix J) confirms the hierarchy holds (DAPT advantage 40–
56 pp over vanilla). Cross-LLM validation shows
jailbreak detection transfers when benign distribution is held constant (F1 recovers to 0.800–1.000),
but benign-side F1 drops to 0.370–0.430 with unfamiliar benign text (Appendix H). The perturbation
evaluation (n=76) remains underpowered.
9

Detection architecture. Binary vs. 9-class equivalence (F1 gap ≤3.9 pp) suggests the binary head
is not the critical differentiator. FITD provides minimal discriminative signal (all variants F1 ≥ 0.990),
serving as confirmation that simple attacks do not
challenge any variant.

714

Encoder scale. Whether findings hold at LLMscale encoders (7B+) remains open; samearchitecture scaling (e.g., DeBERTa-base vs.
DeBERTa-large) is needed to attribute the capacity
effect to scale alone.

720

Scope and single-turn transfer. Only
persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall
within scope; single-turn token-level attacks (Zou
et al., 2023), non-persuasive strategies, and
non-text modalities are not addressed. ToxicChat
performance reflects zero-shot single-turn transfer;
doubling the training corpus drops ToxicChat
macro F1 from 0.596 to 0.341±0.004 while
multi-turn OOD remains stable, confirming the
architecture targets multi-turn detection.

Ethics Statement

738

This work is defensive: it improves detection of
multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing
new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the
persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding
attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects
were involved; all data will be released under CC
BY-NC 4.0.

```text
1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels matching correct within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K \leq 5$). The scrambled-correct equivalence could reflect classification regularizing representations independently of label content, or random permutation over 9 classes preserving enough correlations; distinguishing these requires varying class count. Topic classification occasionally approaches MLM on ActorAttack (0.625 vs.\ 0.893) despite misaligned labels, reflecting domain exposure rather than the topic objective.
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses; one hypothesis is that MLM captures LLM-specific surface patterns while classification extracts style-invariant features. Cross-LLM error decomposition confirms F1 degradation is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text, not missed attacks.
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}
13	Classification supervision reduces variance 4--20$\times$ over MLM on synthetic OOD (3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013).
14	Frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2: frozen MLM outperforms the full pipeline on ActorAttack (0.941 vs.\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen matches (0.972; Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Encoder capacity, temporal, and lexical analysis.}
17	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. Mean pooling matches BiGRU and shuffling produces $|\Delta| < 1$\,pp (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}), confirming order-invariant detection. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD) demonstrates beyond-lexical features (Section~\ref{sec:limitations}).
18	
19	\section{Conclusion}
20	
21	Classification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders; unsupervised pretraining closes $\sim$60\,pp but collapses under mixed-LLM training. Within the architectures tested, larger encoders need less adaptation; 4--20$\times$ variance reduction reverses on human data. Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter, and continued pretraining is 266$\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).
22	
23	\section{Limitations}
24	\label{sec:limitations}
25	
26	\paragraph{Multi-turn human validation.}
27	All multi-turn findings are derived from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists for evaluation. Whether the evidence hierarchy and classification supervision advantages generalize to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question.
28	
29	\paragraph{Turn-count distribution.}
30	Benign conversations contain 12--20 turns, DD jailbreaks 6--10, and ActorAttack jailbreaks 3--8; the three categories have non-overlapping turn counts. Three observations argue against length as the primary detection cue: (a) early detection at equal-length prefixes $K$=1 and $K$=2 achieves AA OOD F1\,=\,0.773 and 0.935 (Appendix~\ref{tab:actorattack_full}); (b) vanilla models access the same length signal but achieve near-zero OOD recall; and (c) truncating benign conversations to 3 turns yields FPR\,=\,0\% (Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}).
31	
32	\paragraph{Temporal aggregation dilution.}
33	When subtle attacks (ActorAttack) are followed by extended benign padding, BiGRU temporal aggregation dilutes attack signals (F1 drops from 0.963 to 0.383; Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}); in practice, adversaries would not dilute their own successful attacks.
34	
35	\paragraph{OOD benign distribution.}
```

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch] Round 7 审稿修复 — DDL 2026-05-25，EMNLP 2026 ARR May。这次不用验证引用。\n\n## 背景\nRound 7 fresh 审稿 3.0 (Findings)。以下 5 个优先修改项需要在一个 Patch 中完成。Agent 正在并行提取 seed-level F1 数据（用于 Appendix 新表和 Cohen's d），你先做不依赖这些数据的修改，Agent 数据到了再补表。\n\n## 修改清单（按优先级）\n\n### P1: MF2+SF2 — Claim 降级 + 方差限定\n\n**abstract.tex L8:**\n- \"Classification supervision reduces prediction variance 4--20$\\times$ on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds) regardless of label correctness\"\n- → \"Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds), with limited sensitivity to label correctness\"\n- 删除 \"4--20×\" 具体数字（移到 Section 4.3 正文）\n\n**conclusion.tex L21:**\n- \"Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter\"\n- → \"Three negative results---label correctness (descriptive equivalence at n=3), temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter\"\n\n**experiments.tex Section 4.3（TOST 讨论处）:**\n- TOST 结论改为 \"descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence\"\n- 保留 \"4--20× observed range\" 在此处但标注 \"formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at n=3\"\n- 当 Agent 提供 Cohen's d 后，加一句 \"Cohen's d = X.XX (DD) / X.XX (AA), indicating [small/medium] effect size\"\n\n### P2: MF4 — Contribution 重写\n\n**introduction.tex L22-29（contribution 列表）:**\n\n重写三条 contribution：\n\nContribution 1（核心 + supplementary findings）:\n- 保持 DAPT hierarchy 为核心发现（classification-based DAPT 最 robust）\n- 添加 null effects 为 supplementary findings：用 \"We report three counter-intuitive findings\" 而非 \"We establish\"\n- 措辞：用 \"findings\" 而非 \"effects\"\n- 草案方向：\"**Systematic decomposition identifying the most robust adaptation and three counter-intuitive findings.** Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations on synthetic multi-turn data shows classification-based DAPT is the most robust variant; three supplementary findings challenge common assumptions: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels (descriptive evidence at n=3 seeds), temporal order is irrelevant ($\\Delta < 1$\\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.\"\n\nContribution 2:\n- \"Scale-dependent observation\" → \"Architecture- and scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation\"\n- 其余保持\n\nContribution 3:\n- 保持但加一句 practical contribution：推理效率 266× faster + 数据效率 N=50 AA=0.969\n\n### P3: MF1 — Bootstrap 池化 caveat\n\n**experiments.tex L26:**\n- \"10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across seeds\"\n- → \"10,000 resamples over individual predictions (pooled across 3 seeds; this assumes cross-seed independence and is exploratory---seed-level comparisons in Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_level} confirm all pairwise conclusions hold)\"\n\n**experiments.tex L26 后新增一句:**\n- \"Seed-level F1 values (Table~\\ref{tab:seed_level}, Appendix) confirm that the relative ordering of variants is consistent across individual seeds.\"\n\n**Appendix 新增 section \\label{app:seed_level}:**\n- 标题 \"Seed-Level F1 Comparisons\"\n- 占位：Agent 提供 LaTeX 表格后 include（表格文件在 `/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/round7_seed_table.tex`，但你可以先创建 section 结构和说明文字）\n\n### P4: MF3+MF5 — Discussion 新段 \"Benign distribution bottleneck\"\n\n**conclusion.tex（Discussion section）:**\n在现有 4 段后新增第 5 段：\n\n\\paragraph{Benign distribution as the remaining bottleneck.}\n- 核心论点：OOD F1 是 upper bound（benign 为 IID），WildChat FPR=68.1% 证明 benign distribution shift 是实际部署的主要障碍\n- 提及 cross-LLM FP collapse（Table 12）是 benign-side shift 证据\n- 限定：所有核心结论适用于 \"synthetic multi-turn jailbreaks; whether findings generalize to human-authored multi-turn attacks is the primary open question\"\n- **不要**引入 \"negative template\" 术语，只说 benign distribution shift\n- 约 6-8 行\n\n同时检查 Limitations section——如果有与此新 Discussion 段重复的 WildChat 内容，精简 Limitations 中的版本避免冗余。\n\n### P5: SF5 — 术语定义\n\n**method.tex 或 experiments.tex 开头（Section 3 或 Section 4 开头）:**\n新增一小段术语定义（2-3 句）：\n- \"cross-attack OOD\": 在未见于训练的攻击家族上评估，benign 分布保持 IID\n- \"DAPT\": 本文中指所有 Stage-1 适配（含有监督和无监督变体），而非仅 Gururangan et al. [Director 指令] [Patch] Round 7 审稿修复 — DDL 2026-05-25，EMNLP 2026 ARR May。这次不用验证引用。

## 背景
Round 7 fresh 审稿 3.0 (Findings)。以下 5 个优先修改项需要在一个 Patch 中完成。Agent 正在并行提取 seed-level F1 数据（用于 Appendix 新表和 Cohen's d），你先做不依赖这些数据的修改，Agent 数据到了再补表。

## 修改清单（按优先级）

### P1: MF2+SF2 — Claim 降级 + 方差限定

**abstract.tex L8:**
- "Classification supervision reduces prediction variance 4--20$\times$ on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds) regardless of label correctness"
- → "Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds), with limited sensitivity to label correctness"
- 删除 "4--20×" 具体数字（移到 Section 4.3 正文）

**conclusion.tex L21:**
- "Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter"
- → "Three negative results---label correctness (descriptive equivalence at n=3), temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter"

**experiments.tex Section 4.3（TOST 讨论处）:**
- TOST 结论改为 "descriptive evidence suggesting equivalence"
- 保留 "4--20× observed range" 在此处但标注 "formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at n=3"
- 当 Agent 提供 Cohen's d 后，加一句 "Cohen's d = X.XX (DD) / X.XX (AA), indicating [small/medium] effect size"

### P2: MF4 — Contribution 重写

**introduction.tex L22-29（contribution 列表）:**

重写三条 contribution：

Contribution 1（核心 + supplementary findings）:
- 保持 DAPT hierarchy 为核心发现（classification-based DAPT 最 robust）
- 添加 null effects 为 supplementary findings：用 "We report three counter-intuitive findings" 而非 "We establish"
- 措辞：用 "findings" 而非 "effects"
- 草案方向："**Systematic decomposition identifying the most robust adaptation and three counter-intuitive findings.** Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations on synthetic multi-turn data shows classification-based DAPT is the most robust variant; three supplementary findings challenge common assumptions: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels (descriptive evidence at n=3 seeds), temporal order is irrelevant ($\Delta < 1$\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection."

Contribution 2:
- "Scale-dependent observation" → "Architecture- and scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation"
- 其余保持

Contribution 3:
- 保持但加一句 practical contribution：推理效率 266× faster + 数据效率 N=50 AA=0.969

### P3: MF1 — Bootstrap 池化 caveat

**experiments.tex L26:**
- "10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across seeds"
- → "10,000 resamples over individual predictions (pooled across 3 seeds; this assumes cross-seed independence and is exploratory---seed-level comparisons in Appendix~\ref{app:seed_level} confirm all pairwise conclusions hold)"

**experiments.tex L26 后新增一句:**
- "Seed-level F1 values (Table~\ref{tab:seed_level}, Appendix) confirm that the relative ordering of variants is consistent across individual seeds."

**Appendix 新增 section \label{app:seed_level}:**
- 标题 "Seed-Level F1 Comparisons"
- 占位：Agent 提供 LaTeX 表格后 include（表格文件在 `/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/round7_seed_table.tex`，但你可以先创建 section 结构和说明文字）

### P4: MF3+MF5 — Discussion 新段 "Benign distribution bottleneck"

**conclusion.tex（Discussion section）:**
在现有 4 段后新增第 5 段：

\paragraph{Benign distribution as the remaining bottleneck.}
- 核心论点：OOD F1 是 upper bound（benign 为 IID），WildChat FPR=68.1% 证明 benign distribution shift 是实际部署的主要障碍
- 提及 cross-LLM FP collapse（Table 12）是 benign-side shift 证据
- 限定：所有核心结论适用于 "synthetic multi-turn jailbreaks; whether findings generalize to human-authored multi-turn attacks is the primary open question"
- **不要**引入 "negative template" 术语，只说 benign distribution shift
- 约 6-8 行

同时检查 Limitations section——如果有与此新 Discussion 段重复的 WildChat 内容，精简 Limitations 中的版本避免冗余。

### P5: SF5 — 术语定义

**method.tex 或 experiments.tex 开头（Section 3 或 Section 4 开头）:**
新增一小段术语定义（2-3 句）：
- "cross-attack OOD": 在未见于训练的攻击家族上评估，benign 分布保持 IID
- "DAPT": 本文中指所有 Stage-1 适配（含有监督和无监督变体），而非仅 Gururangan et al. (2020) 的无监督 continued pretraining
- "evidence hierarchy": 简要引入（指 vanilla < MLM < classification 的性能梯度），详细定义在 Section 4.3

### 附加修改（与上述同时完成）

**Missing References:**
- **GradSafe (Xie et al., 2024)**: Related Work paragraph 2 添加，标注与本文的方法论交集（representation-level safety signal analysis）。bib 条目：Xie et al., "GradSafe: Detecting Jailbreak Prompts in LLM's Gradient Space", ACL 2024 Findings.
- **SafeDecoding (Xu et al., 2024)**: Related Work paragraph 2 简要提及。
- **Circuit Breakers (Zou et al., 2024)**: Related Work paragraph 2 简要提及。
- 注意：这些 bib 条目需要你查找正确信息后添加到 references.bib。

**M2: devlin2019bert 孤立引用:**
- Related Work paragraph 2 中 DeepContext 处自然引用："DeepContext pairs fine-tuned BERT~\citep{devlin2019bert} with a multi-layer GRU"

**SF6（轻量级）:**
- Discussion "Topic shortcut and label equivalence" 段落中，如果还没有，加 1-2 句 mechanistic hypothesis："One hypothesis is that classification loss regularizes the representation space independently of label content, preventing catastrophic drift from the pretrained manifold."
- 不要写成独立段落。

## 约束
- 8 页正文限制（EMNLP 2026），unlimited appendix
- experiments.tex L27 已有 "Unless otherwise noted, all standard deviations..."——不要改这句
- conclusion.tex L21 已有 "On synthetic multi-turn evaluations,"——不要改这个前缀
- Discussion 从 4 段变 5 段后确认仍在 page 8 内
- 完成后 compile 验证

1	% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.
4	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, and existing defenses fail to generalize across attack families.
5	We isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.
6	On single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\,=\,0.818 vs.\ 0.291 without adaptation).
7	Across three architectures (125--355M), the set of attacks requiring adaptation shrinks with scale, though architecture and pretraining differences prevent isolating scale as the sole factor.
8	Classification supervision reduces prediction variance 4--20$\times$ on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds) regardless of label correctness, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.
9	These findings show that the adaptation mechanism, not just domain exposure, determines cross-condition robustness in multi-turn jailbreak detection.
10	\end{abstract}
11

```text
1	% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	
5	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.
6	
7	Existing defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and therefore miss manipulation that unfolds across exchanges. Multi-turn defenses have begun to model conversation-level structure: DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026} pairs fine-tuned BERT with a multi-layer GRU, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, and G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025} applies graph neural networks. These systems achieve strong in-distribution performance, but each is evaluated on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}
8	
9	Pretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\ DeepContext) drops from F1\,=\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.
10	
11	Our pipeline, \textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.
12	
13	\begin{figure}[t]
14	\centering
15	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}
16	\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}
17	\label{fig:architecture}
18	\end{figure}
19	
20	Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.289 $\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. A 4--20$\times$ variance reduction is observed for classification supervision on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
21	
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations, three OOD attack families, and two training compositions on synthetic multi-turn data identifies classification-based domain adaptation as the most robust variant; unsupervised pretraining is effective on single-source data but collapses on mixed-LLM training. Three negative results are counter-intuitive: scrambled labels match correct labels (TOST-confirmed on DD; underpowered on ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant, and label granularity does not affect detection.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation.} Three encoder scales (125M, 184M, 355M) suggest adaptation need shrinks with model size, though encoders differ in architecture; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate scale. RoBERTa-large handles structurally complex attacks without adaptation (Section~\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).
27	
28	  \item \textbf{Condition-dependent adaptation effects.} A variance reduction of 4--20$\times$ is observed (3 seeds) on synthetic data, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data. Mixed-LLM training validates that classification-based DAPT remains the strongest variant across training compositions, though the magnitude of advantage varies by attack complexity (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). The 9-class taxonomy serves as interpretability tool and ablation infrastructure for label quality assessment (Section~\ref{sec:strategy}).
29	\end{itemize}
30	
```

22	
23	\paragraph{Metrics.}
24	F1 for binary jailbreak detection at prefix $K$=2 and full conversations (full per-$K$ results in Appendix~\ref{sec:full_prefix}).
25	OOD results report 3-seed mean$\pm$std (seeds 42, 123, 456) for all variants.
26	For key pairwise comparisons, we report BCa bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across seeds) and paired permutation tests (10,000 permutations). Bootstrap means may differ slightly from seed-averaged F1 due to the nonlinearity of F1 aggregation.
27	All standard deviations are computed over 3 random seeds using the population formula ($n$ divisor); Bessel-corrected estimates ($n{-}1$ divisor) are $\approx$22\% larger but do not alter any significance conclusions.
28	Stage-1: learning rate $2 \times 10^{-5}$, batch 16, 4 epochs, MLM masking 15\%.
29	Stage-2: two-layer MLP, 20 epochs.
30	
31	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
32	\subsection{Cross-Attack Generalization}
33	
34	\begin{table}[t]
35	\centering
36	\small

```text
1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels matching correct within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K \leq 5$). The scrambled-correct equivalence could reflect classification regularizing representations independently of label content, or random permutation over 9 classes preserving enough correlations; distinguishing these requires varying class count. Topic classification occasionally approaches MLM on ActorAttack (0.625 vs.\ 0.893) despite misaligned labels, reflecting domain exposure rather than the topic objective.
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses; one hypothesis is that MLM captures LLM-specific surface patterns while classification extracts style-invariant features. Cross-LLM error decomposition confirms F1 degradation is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text, not missed attacks.
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}
13	Classification supervision reduces variance 4--20$\times$ over MLM on synthetic OOD (3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013).
14	Frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2: frozen MLM outperforms the full pipeline on ActorAttack (0.941 vs.\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen matches (0.972; Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Encoder capacity, temporal, and lexical analysis.}
17	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. Mean pooling matches BiGRU and shuffling produces $|\Delta| < 1$\,pp (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}), confirming order-invariant detection. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD) demonstrates beyond-lexical features (Section~\ref{sec:limitations}).
18	
19	\section{Conclusion}
20	
21	Classification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders; unsupervised pretraining closes $\sim$60\,pp but collapses under mixed-LLM training. Within the architectures tested, larger encoders need less adaptation; 4--20$\times$ variance reduction reverses on human data. Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter, and continued pretraining is 266$\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).
22	
23	\section{Limitations}
24	\label{sec:limitations}
25	
```

Based on the search results, here's the information about the GradSafe paper:

## Paper Details

The paper "GradSafe: Detecting Jailbreak Prompts for LLMs via Safety-Critical Gradient Analysis" was authored by Yueqi Xie, Minghong Fang, Renjie Pi, and Neil Gong. 

## Publication Information

The paper was published in the Proceedings of the 62nd Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics (Volume 1: Long Papers) on pages 507–518, Bangkok, Thailand.

## Research Overview

GradSafe effectively detects jailbreak prompts by scrutinizing the gradients of safety-critical parameters in LLMs. When applied to Llama-2 without further training, GradSafe outperforms Llama Guard—despite its extensive finetuning with a large dataset—in detecting jailbreak prompts, with superior performance consistent across both zero-shot and adaptation scenarios.

Note: The search results show this was published at ACL 2024 (the main conference), not ACL Findings. The source code is available at https://github.com/xyq7/GradSafe.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

```text
1	% related_work.tex — D048 deep writing optimization
2	
3	\section{Related Work}
4	
5	\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}
6	All multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj}, yet each operationalizes it differently: Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} gradually escalates, FITD~\citep{fitd2024} exploits commitment-consistency bias, DD~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} combines emotional manipulation with role assignment, ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} steers through multiple personas, and automated methods (PAIR~\citep{chao2023pair}, TAP~\citep{mehrotra2024tap}) iteratively refine adversarial prompts, extending the automated red-teaming paradigm~\citep{perez2022redteaming}. \citet{wei2023jailbroken} provide a unifying taxonomy; RED QUEEN~\citep{jiang2024redqueen} constructs concealed multi-turn scenarios across 14 harmful categories; benchmarks~\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench} standardize single-turn comparison, while MultiBreak~\citep{song2026multibreak} targets multi-turn evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our evaluation: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.
7	
8	\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}
9	No existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Recent conversation-level systems pair fine-tuned encoders with sequence-level aggregation via GRU hierarchies (DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}), turn-intention inference (BIID~\citep{tong2025biid}), graph neural networks (G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025}), or predictive generation (SafeDream~\citep{safedream2026}), yet each evaluates on a single attack family. Representation-level approaches include X-Boundary~\citep{lu2025xboundary}, JBShield~\citep{zhang2025jbshield} via activated concept analysis, latent activation probing~\citep{kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace}, and vision-language detection~\citep{hua2026rcs}; these require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Our work uses black-box classification with systematic cross-attack OOD evaluation. Per-turn classifiers (Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024}, ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, Constitutional Classifiers~\citep{anthropic2025constitutional}) cannot model multi-turn trajectories, and perturbation-based defenses~\citep{robey2024smoothllm} target single-turn token-level attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer.
10	
11	\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}
12	Persuasion and discourse frameworks offer interpretable vocabulary for attack strategies, but whether such structure aids detection has not been empirically disentangled. Foundational work (RST~\citep{mann1988rst}, PDTB~\citep{prasad2008pdtb}, dialogue acts~\citep{stolcke2000dialogueact}, Speech Act Theory~\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts}) models pragmatic structure at different granularities. Building on Cialdini's principles~\citep{cialdini2001influence}, PAP~\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\% of successful attacks; propaganda analysis~\citep{dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval} and persuasive dialogue~\citep{wang2019persuasion} apply similar technique-level classification. Our ablation disentangles these roles: the taxonomy enables interpretable strategy characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness.
13	
14	\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}
15	Continued pretraining on unlabeled domain text yields consistent downstream gains~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}, extending the pretrain-then-fine-tune approach of ULMFiT~\citep{howard2018ulmfit}, yet this mechanism has not been disentangled for safety-critical generalization. Intermediate-task transfer (STILTs~\citep{phang2018stilts}) and multi-task learning~\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} offer complementary paths but confound domain exposure with supervised signal. Our MLM control removes all classification labels and confirms that pretraining alone closes the OOD gap. Dataset artifact analysis~\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing} motivates our TF-IDF baseline, adversarial NLP methods~\citep{jin2020textfooler,li2020bertattack,nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive} inform the perturbation protocol, and probing techniques~\citep{conneau2018probing,hewitt2019structural} underpin the CKA analysis.
16	
```

_source line 2155 · 2026-05-21T07:32:18.035Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012Aiq94zEfuwBn9HiHrYknb`</summary>

```text
100	
101	A clear \emph{evidence hierarchy} emerges across seven variants (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}), ordered by OOD detection at full conversation length on DD:
102	
103	\vspace{2pt}
104	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026) $<$ topic (0.541$\pm$0.207) $\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) $\approx$ jb-MLM (0.914$\pm$0.115) $<$ scrambled (0.994$\pm$0.005) $=$ 9-class (0.994$\pm$0.005) $\approx$ binary (1.000$\pm$0.000)}
105	\vspace{2pt}
106	
107	\paragraph{Domain exposure is the primary mechanism.}
108	MLM bridges $\sim$60\,pp from vanilla (0.312$\pm$0.026 $\to$ 0.914$\pm$0.115) without labels; this supports the DAPT interpretation~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 9-class 0.972) confirms that domain-adapted representations capture features beyond lexical overlap.
109	All three classification-supervised variants converge to equivalent detection (F1 = 0.994--1.000); McNemar's ($p > 0.13$) and paired permutation tests ($p \geq 0.40$) confirm equivalence. TOST equivalence tests (margin = 5\,pp) confirm equivalence on DD ($p < 0.01$; Table~\ref{tab:tost_sensitivity}); on ActorAttack, TOST is underpowered (SE\,=\,0.045) despite a 2.1\,pp mean gap. Label quality and granularity are non-factors for detection.
110	Classification supervision reduces cross-seed variance 4--20$\times$ over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\ 0.115 on DD; observed across 3 seeds, suggestive but not conclusive; cross-seed ensemble yields marginal additional gains, Appendix~\ref{app:ensemble}). This stability benefit does not transfer to human-authored data, where classification-supervised variants exhibit the highest variance and pure MLM the lowest (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
111	Appendix~\ref{app:tsne} provides t-SNE visualizations confirming that domain-adapted variants produce tighter, more separable embedding clusters than the vanilla baseline.
112	
113	\paragraph{Domain specificity is marginal at full length but meaningful for early detection.}
114	Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length (1\,pp gap), though jailbreak-domain text provides an 8\,pp advantage at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911).
115	Perturbation analysis (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}) reveals a sharper domain specificity effect: Wikipedia MLM degrades by $\sim$12\,pp under adversarial paraphrasing while jailbreak-domain MLM degrades by only 2.6\,pp.
116	
117	\paragraph{Misaligned supervision destabilizes generalization.}
118	Topic classification yields DD-Full F1 = 0.541$\pm$0.207; seed 123 falls \emph{below} vanilla (0.252 vs.\ 0.312). Despite using the same jailbreak-domain training data, topic classification performs worse than Wikipedia MLM (0.904$\pm$0.115), which uses out-of-domain text with no labels. A misaligned objective forces the encoder to allocate capacity to irrelevant features and actively destabilizes representations that MLM would preserve.
119	
```

</details>

Based on my search results, here's information about the Circuit Breakers paper:

## Circuit Breakers: Zou et al. 2024

The "Circuit Breakers" paper was published in June 2024 and authored by Andy Zou, Long Phan, Justin Wang, Derek Duenas, Maxwell Lin, Maksym Andriushchenko, Rowan Wang, Zico Kolter, Matt Fredrikson, and Dan Hendrycks.

### What are Circuit Breakers?

Zou et al. (2024) developed a RepE-based jailbreak mitigation called "circuit breakers," which aim to interrupt a model's generation of harmful content while preserving desirable model behaviors. 

As an alternative to refusal training and adversarial training, circuit-breaking directly controls the representations that are responsible for harmful outputs in the first place.

### How They Work

The model is fine-tuned to modify its representations of harmful inputs such that they are orthogonal to the frozen model's representations of the same inputs.

### Effectiveness and Limitations

The technique can be applied to both text-only and multimodal language models to prevent the generation of harmful outputs without sacrificing utility—even in the presence of powerful unseen attacks, and circuit breakers allow the larger multimodal system to reliably withstand image "hijacks".

However, circuit breakers remain susceptible to multi-turn jailbreaks, with research showing that multi-turn jailbreaks were able to bypass this mitigation.

Based on the web search results, here is the information about the SafeDecoding paper:

**Paper Title:** SafeDecoding: Defending against Jailbreak Attacks via Safety-Aware Decoding

**Authors:** Zhangchen Xu, Fengqing Jiang, Luyao Niu, Jinyuan Jia, Bill Yuchen Lin, and Radha Poovendran

**Publication:** Published by the Association for Computational Linguistics (ACL) in 2024

**Main Contribution:** SafeDecoding is a safety-aware decoding strategy for LLMs to generate helpful and harmless responses to user queries

**Core Approach:** The method is based on the observation that safety disclaimers appear among the top tokens, and works by identifying safety disclaimers and amplifying their token probabilities, while simultaneously attenuating harmful token sequences

**Results:** SafeDecoding significantly reduces attack success rate and harmfulness of jailbreak attacks without compromising the helpfulness of responses to benign user queries while outperforming six defense methods (Perpelexity, Paraphrase, Retokenization, Self-Reminder, ICD, and Self-Examination)

    "originalFile": "% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\\begin{abstract}\nDetecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, and existing defenses fail to generalize across attack families.\nWe isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.\nOn single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\\,=\\,0.818 vs.\\ 0.291 without adaptation).\nAcross three architectures (125--355M), the set of attacks requiring adaptation shrinks with scale, though architecture and pretraining differences prevent isolating scale as the sole factor.\nClassification supervision reduces prediction variance 4--20$\\times$ on synthetic evaluations (observed across 3 seeds) regardless of label correctness, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.\nThese findings show that the adaptation mechanism, not just domain exposure, determines cross-condition robustness in multi-turn jailbreak detection.\n\\end{abstract}\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "c4177c7d-96ef-4e46-9294-15bcee06e45b",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "89b74e66-1fcb-46b7-846c-68b951f64556",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "1793ba5d-2556-44f1-913a-5683d7126a87",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper/conclusion.tex",
    "oldString": "Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter, and continued pretraining",
    "newString": "Three negative results---label correctness (descriptive equivalence at $n$=3), temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter, and continued pretraining",
    "originalFile": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels matching correct within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$). The scrambled-correct equivalence could reflect classification regularizing representations independently of label content, or random permutation over 9 classes preserving enough correlations; distinguishing these requires varying class count. Topic classification occasionally approaches MLM on ActorAttack (0.625 vs.\\ 0.893) despite misaligned labels, reflecting domain exposure rather than the topic objective.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}\nToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 (+53\\,pp) while MLM collapses; one hypothesis is that MLM captures LLM-specific surface patterns while classification extracts style-invariant features. Cross-LLM error decomposition confirms F1 degradation is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text, not missed attacks.\n\n\\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}\nClassification supervision reduces variance 4--20$\\times$ over MLM on synthetic OOD (3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013).\nFrozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2: frozen MLM outperforms the full pipeline on ActorAttack (0.941 vs.\\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen matches (0.972; Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}).\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder capacity, temporal, and lexical analysis.}\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. Mean pooling matches BiGRU and shuffling produces $|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}), confirming order-invariant detection. TF-IDF collapse on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD) demonstrates beyond-lexical features (Section~\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nClassification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders; unsupervised pretraining closes $\\sim$60\\,pp but collapses under mixed-LLM training. Within the architectures tested, larger encoders need less adaptation; 4--20$\\times$ variance reduction reverses on human data. Three negative results---label correctness, temporal order, domain specificity---constrain which properties matter, and continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), offering a practical alternative given matched benign distributions (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}).\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn human validation.}\nAll multi-turn findings are derived from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists for evaluation. Whether the evidence hierarchy and classification supervision advantages generalize to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question.\n\n\\paragraph{Turn-count distribution.}\nBenign conversations contain 12--20 turns, DD jailbreaks 6--10, and ActorAttack jailbreaks 3--8; the three categories have non-overlapping turn counts. Three observations argue against length as the primary detection cue: (a) early detection at equal-length prefixes $K$=1 and $K$=2 achieves AA OOD F1\\,=\\,0.773 and 0.935 (Appendix~\\ref{tab:actorattack_full}); (b) vanilla models access the same length signal but achieve near-zero OOD recall; and (c) truncating benign conversations to 3 turns yields FPR\\,=\\,0\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal aggregation dilution.}\nWhen subtle attacks (ActorAttack) are followed by extended benign padding, BiGRU temporal aggregation dilutes attack signals (F1 drops from 0.963 to 0.383; Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}); in practice, adversaries would not dilute their own successful attacks.\n\n\\paragraph{OOD benign distribution.}\nAll OOD evaluations hold the benign distribution constant (Qwen3-8B generated); results reflect attack-side generalization. On 226 WildChat benign conversations, all variants produce FPR\\,$\\geq$\\,44\\%, with 9-class worst at 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). Even with matched benign distributions, residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\n\\paragraph{Frozen encoder assumption.}\nThe frozen Stage-1 design isolates representation quality but may underestimate full-pipeline performance. End-to-end fine-tuning closes this gap to $\\leq$5.8\\,pp on ActorAttack (F1\\,=\\,0.997$\\pm$0.005 vs.\\ 0.939$\\pm$0.047 frozen; Table~\\ref{tab:e2e}; Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}).\n\n\\paragraph{Dataset scale and cross-LLM transfer.}\nScale validation (1{,}000 conversations; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_validation}) confirms the hierarchy holds (DAPT advantage 40--56\\,pp over vanilla). Cross-LLM validation shows jailbreak detection transfers when benign distribution is held constant (F1 recovers to 0.800--1.000), but benign-side F1 drops to 0.370--0.430 with unfamiliar benign text (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}). The perturbation evaluation ($n$=76) remains underpowered.\n\n\\paragraph{Strategy labels.}\nPersuasion strategy labels ($n$=50 human validation, $\\kappa$=0.78) are produced by Qwen3-8B; the validation sample yields small per-class sizes across 9 categories, limiting reliability estimates for individual strategies. OOD strategy accuracy degrades to 30.7\\% ($\\kappa$=0.126); interpretability is limited to known attack families. We plan to release annotation prompts and data for independent verification.\n\n\\paragraph{Detection architecture.}\nBinary vs.\\ 9-class equivalence (F1 gap $\\leq$3.9\\,pp) suggests the binary head is not the critical differentiator. FITD provides minimal discriminative signal (all variants F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.990), serving as confirmation that simple attacks do not challenge any variant.\n\n\\paragraph{Encoder scale.}\nWhether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open; same-architecture scaling (e.g., DeBERTa-base vs.\\ DeBERTa-large) is needed to attribute the capacity effect to scale alone.\n\n\\paragraph{Scope and single-turn transfer.}\nOnly persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. ToxicChat performance reflects zero-shot single-turn transfer; doubling the training corpus drops ToxicChat macro F1 from 0.596 to 0.341$\\pm$0.004 while multi-turn OOD remains stable, confirming the architecture targets multi-turn detection.\n\n\\paragraph{Computation cost.}\nTotal pipeline cost for all seven variants is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",

660	\bottomrule
661	\end{tabular}
662	\end{table}
663	
664	Table~\ref{tab:ensemble} shows that majority vote never corrupts correct predictions (Broke\,=\,0 for all variants). MLM benefits most from ensembling (+8.6\,pp DD, +6.0\,pp AA), because its per-seed errors are complementary---each seed fails on different samples, and the majority corrects these. This further exposes MLM's seed instability: its individual seeds are unreliable, but their errors are uncorrelated. By contrast, 9-class seeds are already highly consistent (std\,$\leq$\,0.026), leaving minimal room for ensemble improvement (+0.3--0.9\,pp). Vanilla errors are systematic (all seeds fail on the same OOD samples), so ensembling provides no benefit.
665

  "old_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:ensemble} shows that majority vote never corrupts correct predictions (Broke\\,=\\,0 for all variants). MLM benefits most from ensembling (+8.6\\,pp DD, +6.0\\,pp AA), because its per-seed errors are complementary---each seed fails on different samples, and the majority corrects these. This further exposes MLM's seed instability: its individual seeds are unreliable, but their errors are uncorrelated. By contrast, 9-class seeds are already highly consistent (std\\,$\\leq$\\,0.026), leaving minimal room for ensemble improvement (+0.3--0.9\\,pp). Vanilla errors are systematic (all seeds fail on the same OOD samples), so ensembling provides no benefit.",
  "new_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:ensemble} shows that majority vote never corrupts correct predictions (Broke\\,=\\,0 for all variants). MLM benefits most from ensembling (+8.6\\,pp DD, +6.0\\,pp AA), because its per-seed errors are complementary---each seed fails on different samples, and the majority corrects these. This further exposes MLM's seed instability: its individual seeds are unreliable, but their errors are uncorrelated. By contrast, 9-class seeds are already highly consistent (std\\,$\\leq$\\,0.026), leaving minimal room for ensemble improvement (+0.3--0.9\\,pp). Vanilla errors are systematic (all seeds fail on the same OOD samples), so ensembling provides no benefit.\n\n\\section{Seed-Level F1 Comparisons}\n\\label{app:seed_level}\n\nTo verify that cross-seed pooling does not mask inconsistent pairwise orderings, Table~\\ref{tab:seed_level} reports per-seed F1 for all variants on OOD DD and ActorAttack at full conversation length. The relative ordering of variants is consistent across all three seeds: classification-supervised variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) dominate MLM and vanilla at every seed, and no seed reverses the hierarchy established by the pooled analysis.\n\n% Placeholder: seed-level F1 table to be inserted when data available\n% \\input{seed_level_table}\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Per-seed F1 on OOD Full for key variants (seeds 42, 123, 456). Label quality and granularity are descriptively non-factors for detection.\nClassification supervision is associated with 4--20$\\times$ lower cross-seed variance over MLM on synthetic OOD (std 0.005 vs.\\ 0.115 on DD; observed range across 3 seeds; formal variance ratio tests are not applicable at $n$=3; cross-seed ensemble yields marginal additional gains, Appendix~\\ref{app:ensemble})."

    "newString": "Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).",
    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and therefore miss manipulation that unfolds across exchanges. Multi-turn defenses have begun to model conversation-level structure: DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026} pairs fine-tuned BERT with a multi-layer GRU, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, and G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025} applies graph neural networks. These systems achieve strong in-distribution performance, but each is evaluated on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. A 4--20$\\times$ variance reduction is observed for classification supervision on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations, three OOD attack families, and two training compositions on synthetic multi-turn data identifies classification-based domain adaptation as the most robust variant; unsupervised pretraining is effective on single-source data but collapses on mixed-LLM training. Three negative results are counter-intuitive: scrambled labels match correct labels (TOST-confirmed on DD; underpowered on ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant, and label granularity does not affect detection.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Scale-dependent observation on domain adaptation.} Three encoder scales (125M, 184M, 355M) suggest adaptation need shrinks with model size, though encoders differ in architecture; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate scale. RoBERTa-large handles structurally complex attacks without adaptation (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Condition-dependent adaptation effects.} A variance reduction of 4--20$\\times$ is observed (3 seeds) on synthetic data, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data. Mixed-LLM training validates that classification-based DAPT remains the strongest variant across training compositions, though the magnitude of advantage varies by attack complexity (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). The 9-class taxonomy serves as interpretability tool and ablation infrastructure for label quality assessment (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",

MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Mixed-LLM training validates that classification-based DAPT remains the strongest variant across training compositions (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). The 9-class taxonomy serves as interpretability tool and ablation infrastructure (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}). Continued pretraining is 266$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge inference and near-saturates at 50 training conversations (AA F1\\,=\\,0.969; Appendix~\\ref{app:data_efficiency})."

    "originalFile": "% related_work.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\n\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}\nAll multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj}, yet each operationalizes it differently: Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} gradually escalates, FITD~\\citep{fitd2024} exploits commitment-consistency bias, DD~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} combines emotional manipulation with role assignment, ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} steers through multiple personas, and automated methods (PAIR~\\citep{chao2023pair}, TAP~\\citep{mehrotra2024tap}) iteratively refine adversarial prompts, extending the automated red-teaming paradigm~\\citep{perez2022redteaming}. \\citet{wei2023jailbroken} provide a unifying taxonomy; RED QUEEN~\\citep{jiang2024redqueen} constructs concealed multi-turn scenarios across 14 harmful categories; benchmarks~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench} standardize single-turn comparison, while MultiBreak~\\citep{song2026multibreak} targets multi-turn evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our evaluation: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}\nNo existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Recent conversation-level systems pair fine-tuned encoders with sequence-level aggregation via GRU hierarchies (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}), turn-intention inference (BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}), graph neural networks (G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}), or predictive generation (SafeDream~\\citep{safedream2026}), yet each evaluates on a single attack family. Representation-level approaches include X-Boundary~\\citep{lu2025xboundary}, JBShield~\\citep{zhang2025jbshield} via activated concept analysis, latent activation probing~\\citep{kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace}, and vision-language detection~\\citep{hua2026rcs}; these require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Our work uses black-box classification with systematic cross-attack OOD evaluation. Per-turn classifiers (Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024}, ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, Constitutional Classifiers~\\citep{anthropic2025constitutional}) cannot model multi-turn trajectories, and perturbation-based defenses~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm} target single-turn token-level attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}\nPersuasion and discourse frameworks offer interpretable vocabulary for attack strategies, but whether such structure aids detection has not been empirically disentangled. Foundational work (RST~\\citep{mann1988rst}, PDTB~\\citep{prasad2008pdtb}, dialogue acts~\\citep{stolcke2000dialogueact}, Speech Act Theory~\\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts}) models pragmatic structure at different granularities. Building on Cialdini's principles~\\citep{cialdini2001influence}, PAP~\\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks; propaganda analysis~\\citep{dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval} and persuasive dialogue~\\citep{wang2019persuasion} apply similar technique-level classification. Our ablation disentangles these roles: the taxonomy enables interpretable strategy characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}\nContinued pretraining on unlabeled domain text yields consistent downstream gains~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}, extending the pretrain-then-fine-tune approach of ULMFiT~\\citep{howard2018ulmfit}, yet this mechanism has not been disentangled for safety-critical generalization. Intermediate-task transfer (STILTs~\\citep{phang2018stilts}) and multi-task learning~\\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} offer complementary paths but confound domain exposure with supervised signal. Our MLM control removes all classification labels and confirms that pretraining alone closes the OOD gap. Dataset artifact analysis~\\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing} motivates our TF-IDF baseline, adversarial NLP methods~\\citep{jin2020textfooler,li2020bertattack,nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive} inform the perturbation protocol, and probing techniques~\\citep{conneau2018probing,hewitt2019structural} underpin the CKA analysis.\n",

Representation-level approaches include X-Boundary~\\citep{lu2025xboundary}, JBShield~\\citep{zhang2025jbshield} via activated concept analysis, gradient-space analysis (GradSafe~\\citep{xie2024gradsafe}), circuit breakers via representation engineering~\\citep{zou2024circuitbreakers}, latent activation probing~\\citep{kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace}, and vision-language detection~\\citep{hua2026rcs}; these require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Our work uses black-box classification with systematic cross-attack OOD evaluation. Per-turn classifiers (Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024}, ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, Constitutional Classifiers~\\citep{anthropic2025constitutional}) cannot model multi-turn trajectories, and perturbation-based defenses~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm} and safety-aware decoding~\\citep{xu2024safedecoding} target single-turn token-level attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}",
          " Persuasion and discourse frameworks offer interpretable vocabulary for attack strategies, but whether such structure aids detection has not been empirically disentangled. Foundational work (RST~\\citep{mann1988rst}, PDTB~\\citep{prasad2008pdtb}, dialogue acts~\\citep{stolcke2000dialogueact}, Speech Act Theory~\\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts}) models pragmatic structure at different granularities. Building on Cialdini's principles~\\citep{cialdini2001influence}, PAP~\\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks; propaganda analysis~\\citep{dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval} and persuasive dialogue~\\citep{wang2019persuasion} apply similar technique-level classification. Our ablation disentangles these roles: the taxonomy enables interpretable strategy characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness."

    "newString": "Representation-level approaches include X-Boundary~\\citep{lu2025xboundary}, JBShield~\\citep{zhang2025jbshield} via activated concept analysis, gradient-space analysis (GradSafe~\\citep{xie2024gradsafe}), circuit breakers via representation engineering~\\citep{zou2024circuitbreakers}, latent activation probing~\\citep{kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace}, and vision-language detection~\\citep{hua2026rcs}; these require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}.",
    "originalFile": "% related_work.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\n\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}\nAll multi-turn jailbreak families share a persuasion substrate~\\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj}, yet each operationalizes it differently: Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} gradually escalates, FITD~\\citep{fitd2024} exploits commitment-consistency bias, DD~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} combines emotional manipulation with role assignment, ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} steers through multiple personas, and automated methods (PAIR~\\citep{chao2023pair}, TAP~\\citep{mehrotra2024tap}) iteratively refine adversarial prompts, extending the automated red-teaming paradigm~\\citep{perez2022redteaming}. \\citet{wei2023jailbroken} provide a unifying taxonomy; RED QUEEN~\\citep{jiang2024redqueen} constructs concealed multi-turn scenarios across 14 harmful categories; benchmarks~\\citep{mazeika2024harmbench,chao2024jailbreakbench} standardize single-turn comparison, while MultiBreak~\\citep{song2026multibreak} targets multi-turn evaluation. This shared substrate with diverse surface strategies defines our evaluation: a detector must generalize across families, not merely succeed on its training distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses and production safety.}\nNo existing defense evaluates cross-attack transfer. Recent conversation-level systems pair fine-tuned BERT~\\citep{devlin2019bert} encoders with sequence-level aggregation via GRU hierarchies (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}), turn-intention inference (BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}), graph neural networks (G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}), or predictive generation (SafeDream~\\citep{safedream2026}), yet each evaluates on a single attack family. Representation-level approaches include X-Boundary~\\citep{lu2025xboundary}, JBShield~\\citep{zhang2025jbshield} via activated concept analysis, latent activation probing~\\citep{kulkarni2026latentadversarial,kadali2026trace}, and vision-language detection~\\citep{hua2026rcs}; these require white-box access and can be brittle under adaptive attacks~\\citep{derya2026revisitingjbshield}. Our work uses black-box classification with systematic cross-attack OOD evaluation. Per-turn classifiers (Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024}, ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, Constitutional Classifiers~\\citep{anthropic2025constitutional}) cannot model multi-turn trajectories, and perturbation-based defenses~\\citep{robey2024smoothllm} target single-turn token-level attacks. Our ablation fills this gap: decomposition across thirteen configurations under OOD evaluation identifies domain exposure as the dominant factor behind cross-attack transfer.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational persuasion and discourse.}\nPersuasion and discourse frameworks offer interpretable vocabulary for attack strategies, but whether such structure aids detection has not been empirically disentangled. Foundational work (RST~\\citep{mann1988rst}, PDTB~\\citep{prasad2008pdtb}, dialogue acts~\\citep{stolcke2000dialogueact}, Speech Act Theory~\\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts}) models pragmatic structure at different granularities. Building on Cialdini's principles~\\citep{cialdini2001influence}, PAP~\\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks; propaganda analysis~\\citep{dasanmartino2019fine,dasanmartino2020semeval} and persuasive dialogue~\\citep{wang2019persuasion} apply similar technique-level classification. Our ablation disentangles these roles: the taxonomy enables interpretable strategy characterization, but detection operates through domain-adapted embeddings regardless of label correctness.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}\nContinued pretraining on unlabeled domain text yields consistent downstream gains~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}, extending the pretrain-then-fine-tune approach of ULMFiT~\\citep{howard2018ulmfit}, yet this mechanism has not been disentangled for safety-critical generalization. Intermediate-task transfer (STILTs~\\citep{phang2018stilts}) and multi-task learning~\\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} offer complementary paths but confound domain exposure with supervised signal. Our MLM control removes all classification labels and confirms that pretraining alone closes the OOD gap. Dataset artifact analysis~\\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing} motivates our TF-IDF baseline, adversarial NLP methods~\\citep{jin2020textfooler,li2020bertattack,nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive} inform the perturbation protocol, and probing techniques~\\citep{conneau2018probing,hewitt2019structural} underpin the CKA analysis.\n",

{
